<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, May 28, 2013  
 
PRESENT: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Supervisor; and Jonathan M. Nez, Chairman were present in person.  
9:00 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Tenney.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments. 
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: May 14, 2013 Regular Meeting and May 20, 2013 Special Meeting b) 
Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 for April 2013 c) Name an existing road in the Pinetop/Lakeside area to 
“Evening Sky Drive” in Section 10, Township 09 North, Range 23 East d) Letter to the Resources and 
Development Committee regarding the Restoration of Priority –N71 e) Amendment 1 to IGA #120067DC with 
the Department of Corrections for correctional officer certification training f) Letter regarding comments on the 
Programmatic Draft Environmental Impact Statement for the Apache -Sitgreaves National Forests Land 
Management Plan g) Approval of the use of the southwest corner of the Navajo County Government Comp lex 
for the annual 4 th of July Fireworks display h) Quit Claim Deed to Christopher J. Fernandes for APN 105 -32-
213 i) Approval of the purchase of Information Technology hardware not to exceed $299,000 j) Contract signed 
by County Manager per Board of Super visors Resolution: Contract for Professional Services between Navajo 
County and Stantec Consulting Services, Inc. and Contract for a Benefit Cost Analysis Agreement between 
Navajo County and Benefit Intelligence, Inc. k) Sympathy letter to the family of th e Honorable Evelyne E. 
Bradley: Supervisor Tenney  made a motion  to authorize the Chairman to sign the consent agenda as 
presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Recognition of Judge Hig gins for receiving the Ignation Award from Santa Clara University : Hunter 
Moore presented a video produced by Santa Clara University  which recognized the Honorable Robert 
Higgins for his outstanding work ; not only his legal a ccomplishments but his founding  of Saint  
Anthony's Catholic School  and his compassion and service to humanity . Supervisor Tenney made a 
motion to approve signing the  letter in recognition of Judge Higgins for receiving the Ignation Award 
from Santa Clara University ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion. Supervisor Whitesinger expressed her gratitude to Judge Higgins for the positive influence 
the school has made in her life and the lives of her children. Supervisor Allen, Supervisor Thompson, 
Supervisor Tenney and Chairman Nez expressed their gratitude and congratulations to Judge Higgins. 
County Attorney Brad Carlyon expressed what a great honor this was for Judge Higgins to receive this 
Ignation Award from Santa Clara and indicated all the other recipients have been  national leaders. He 
spoke about Judge Higgins bringing in quality education to the children of the White Mountains and his 
many accomplishments. County Manager James Jayne  expressed his appreciation and congratulated  
Judge Hi ggins. Judge Higgins thanked the Board of S upervisors, Mr. Carylon and Mr. Jayne. He 
indicated he was very surprised to receive the award and expressed he was very happy to work with St. 
Anthony’s School and indicated this had been a work of love.  
b. Presentation to Chairman Nez for his successful completion of the  2013 Shiprock Marathon & Relay : 
County Manager James Jayne presented Chairman Nez with a plaque for the successful completion of 
the 2013 Shiprock Marathon and Relay.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 05-28-2013 
Page 2 of 5 
 
c. Consideration and possible approval of an Application for Supervised Fireworks Display permit 
submitted by the Heber -Overgaard Chamber of Commerce : Melissa Buckley  discussed the item and 
indicated that the Sheriff’s office has reviewed and approved the application. Supervisor Tenney made 
a motion to approve the Application for Supervised Fireworks Display permit submitted by the Heber -
Overgaard Chamber of Commerce ; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion. 
HUMAN RESOURCES : Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson 
reviewed the 20 item personnel action report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the personnel 
action report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      ASSESSOR: Consideration and possible approval of a settlement agreement in Taylor Park Meadows v. 
Navajo County in TX -2012000409: Cammy Darris discussed the item. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to 
approve the settlement agreement in Taylor Park Meadows v. Navajo County in TX -2012000409; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
SHERIFF: Consideration and possible approval of an Intergovernmental Agreement with the Department of 
Corrections for housing DOC inmates (Sheriff KC Clark) This item was pulled.  
NAVAJO COUNTY LIBRARY DISTRICT Board of Directors Session : Consideration and possible 
approval of the Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Library District and Sequoia Village 
School for the provision of an integrated automated library system : Supervisor Tenney  made a motion  to 
enter into the Navajo County Library District Board of Directions Session ; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Geneva Durkee discussed the item.  Mr. Paul Cryder 
representative of the Sequoia Village School addressed the Board regarding the Library District ’s agreement to 
provide online automated library services to the Sequoia Village School ’s library. He indicated this will allow 
the students to have the advantage to use the large online public access catalog shared by all the public libraries 
in the county.  Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to approve the Memorandum of Und erstanding between 
Navajo County Library District and Sequoia Village School for the provision of an integrated automated library 
system; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen . Vote unanimous approving the motion . Supervisor Tenney  
made a motion  to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.   
PUBLIC WORKS: 
a. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 16-13, approving the submission of a joint 
application on behalf of Navajo County and the Hopi Tribe for funding under the Transportation 
Investments Generating Economic Recovery Competitive Grant Program of the American Recovery and 
Reinvestment Act and approving a letter of support : Norm an Cody discussed the item and requested  
approval. He indicated the purpose of the grant is to construct and pave 13.7 miles of BIA H -60 road 
which will open this main north -south artery for business traffic, such as the delivery of commodities 
and services. Eric Descheenie spoke in regards to th e Resolution which  includes partnerships with the 
Hopi Tribe and the Navajo Nation. He indicated t his has been a collaborative effort  with the 
communities. Supervisor Thompson thanked all those that h ave helped with this project. Supervisor 
Thompson made a motion  to adopt Resolution Number 16 -13, approving the submission  of a joint 
application on behalf of Navajo County and the Hopi Tribe for funding under the Transportation 
Investments Generating Economic Recovery Competitive Grant Program of the American Recovery and

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 05-28-2013 
Page 3 of 5 
 
Reinvestment Act and approving a letter of support ; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of the Memorandum of Understanding between the 
Cornerstone/Basic Faith M inistry and Navajo County : Norman Cody discussed the item and requested 
approval for utilization of the Winslow road yard warehouse as a storage space for the faith based 
humanitarian efforts. Supervisor Thompson indicated that this is a  working relationsh ip of the tribal 
leadership and communities  to improve services  to the impoverished families within  the local areas . 
Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to approve the  Memorandum of Understanding between the 
Cornerstone/Basic Faith Ministry and Navajo County ; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
c. Consideration and possible approval of the Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and 
the Town of Taylor for Flood Mitigation : Bill Bess discussed t he item and asked for approval . Gus 
Lundberg, Taylor Town Manager  and Stu Spalding , Taylor Town Engineer were recognized as being 
present. Gus Lundberg addressed the Board. Supervisor Allen expressed her support but  indicated that 
this item was misplaced under the Public Works Department and should be under the Navajo County 
Flood Control District Board of Directors Session; therefore the item cannot have a final vote today. She 
indicated it will be placed on the June 11, 2013 Board of Supervisors meeting agenda.  The item was 
tabled. 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Allen reported speaking at the Cedar Hills community, attending  the CSA meeting in Phoenix, and 
reported on the AZ, NM  Coalition she attended in New Mexico  where she was elected Vice Presid ent of the 
Coalition. She indicated she visited the Naomi House  where they shelter children who have been in abusive 
situations and also attended the WIR Conference in Flagstaff. On a personal note she reported she attended her 
in-laws 70th wedding anniversary. Supervisor Whitesinger indicated she met with the  Pinetop Lakeside Town 
Council members and received an update on the multi-purpose path which starts from the Billy Creek Bridge to 
the middle school  which she believes will be a good investment for the children of the community to be safe.  
She indicated she had several speaking engag ements including school  graduates and visiting with university 
students who are volunteering at the White Mountain Apache Tr ibe. She reported she gleaned from  the WIR 
conference in Flagstaff  how valuable county government is because of the greater  level to be connected  
personally with the people and communities the counties  serve and represent. Eric Descheenie s hared a quote  
with her by Margaret Mead: “Never doubt that a small group of thoughtful committed citizens can change the 
world; indeed it’s the only thing that ever has.” Supervisor Tenney reported on the meetings he attended: ECO, 
CSA and the WIR conference. He report ed he was recently appointed by CSA to be on the WIR Board of 
Directors to represent the State.  Supervisor Tenney  reported he had  two sons that graduated; one from 8 th 
grade and one from High School. Supervisor Tenney spoke about Memorial Day and wanted to express thanks 
to our veterans who have gone before us and given us the great freedoms we enjoy . Supervisor Thompson 
reported on meeting with Winslow and Navajo Nation officials on a project for those that use alcohol and illegal 
drugs and they discussed possible treatment programs. He continued to discuss several chapter, veterans, 
graduations and legislative meetings that he attended. Brad Carlyon thanked Sheriff Clark for his presentation at 
the WIR Conference on the Cross Commissioning Agreement i n Indian country. He reported being with his 
sons at  the VFW on  Memorial Day  and expressed teaching  the true meaning of the holiday . James Jayne 
reported on his son’s graduation from High School and also Titus Nenning, his exchange student from Austria. 
Mr. Jayne expressed thanks to Terri Hill and others for their service on Memorial Day. Chairman Nez reported 
on District I and indicated that he was the commencement speaker at the Northland Pioneer College graduation. 
He indicated he has been uplifted by atte nding several graduations and promotions. Chairman Nez expressed 
appreciation to the fallen and veterans for their service. He reported on CSA, the budget hearings at the Capitol,

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 05-28-2013 
Page 4 of 5 
 
and reported on his meetings with Senator Jackson and Representative Hale  and expressed support of  Senator 
Jackson’s amendment to the budget which added the Navajo Technical College item to the budget. He reported 
that he and Eric Descheenie met  with Native Home C apital in Phoenix to help get resources to the tribes  for 
housing. He reported Sheriff Clark did a great job with the presentation on Cross Commissioning at WIR where 
the President of the Navajo Nation was there in support . Chairman Nez ran in a Marathon in Long Beach 
California finished in four hours and thirty minutes. Supervisor Thompson and Chairman Nez thanked those 
who represented Navajo County at the funeral of Judge  Evelyne Bradley former Justice of the Peace in Kayenta 
for many years. Hunter Moore reminded all of the Solar Ribbon Cutting ceremony where Congresswom an Ann 
Kirkpatrick will be attending at 10:30 a.m.  
Navajo word of the Day: Silahtso – means an individual that served in the military.  
RECESS: At 10:19 a.m.  
RECONVENE: At 1:01 p.m.  
FINANCE: County Finance Update : James Menlove gave a budget update. He further gave comments 
regarding the general fund budget. 
BUDGET HEARINGS FOR DISCUSSION ONLY:  
a. Assessor: Cammy Darris made a  detailed presentation to the Board  reviewing the Assessor’s 
Department budget. Ms. Darris indicated her department budget is 1.3 million dollars which comes from 
the general fund with no secondary funding.  
b. Constables: James Menlove reviewed the Constable’s budget.  
c. County Attorney: Brad Carlyon discussed the County Attorney’s Department budget indicating that four 
years ago there were 19 attorneys in his office  and now  the number is down to 13  prosecutors. He 
reported the large case load of the judges and the importance of the justice system as being one of the 
fundamental services a government provides. 
d. Recorder/Voter Registration/Elections: Laurette Justman reviewed the Recorder and Voter Registration 
Department budgets. She spoke of the s atellite office in Show Low providing better customer service. 
Johnathan Roes discussed the Elections budget.  
e. Sheriff/Jail: James Menlove discussed the Sheriff’s Department and the Jail operation’s budgets.  
f. Superintendent of Schools: Linda Morrow reviewed the Superintendent of School’s Department budget. 
She indicated there was a $66,889.65 savings in the budget  due to leaving a position open which will be 
filled this year.  
g. Treasurer: Manny Hernandez reviewed the Treasurer’s Department budget.  
h. Judicial Branch/Clerk of the Court : Judge Michala Ruechel discussed the Judicial Branch and Clerk of 
the Court budgets. She spoke of the Superior Court,  the Clerk of the Superior Court and the Justice 
Courts. Supervisor Allen , Supervisor Whitesinger  and Supervisor Tenney acknowledged and 
expressed appreciation to the Courts for their help with the budget.  
i. Workforce Investment Act (WIA): Judy Bratcher discussed the WIA budget.  
j. Administration/Board of Supervisors: James Menlove discussed the Administration/Board of 
Supervisor's Department budget.  
k. Facilities Management: Bret Starns presented the Facilities Management budget.  
l. Navajo County Public Health: Board of Directors’ Session: Supervisor Tenney made a motion to enter 
into the Navajo County Public Health Services District Board of Directors Session ; motion seconded  
by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Dr. Wade Kartchner discussed the

<!-- page 5 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 05-28-2013 
Page 5 of 5 
 
Navajo County Public Health Services District budget. Mary Tyler discussed the charge for birth and 
death c ertificates which is set by st atute. Supervisor Tenney m ade a motion  to return to regular 
session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
m. Information Technology : Robert Heming and Jeff Lineberry reviewed the Information Technology 
budget, discussed their office duties and the new phone system. 
n. Legal Defender: Mariclare Hannah reviewed the Legal Defender’s Department budget.  
o. Navajo County Library District : Board of Directors’ Session : Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to 
enter into the Navajo County Library Distri ct Board of Directors’ Session ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Geneva Durkee discussed the Library 
District’s budget. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
p. Public Defender: Dale Nielson reviewed the Public Defender’s Department b udget. He discussed the 
case numbers and indicated there were 500 cases less than last year. Supervisor Thompson  thanked 
Mr. Nielson for his work.  
q. Public Fiduciary: Sherry Reed reviewed the Public Fiduciary ’s Department budget. She indicated that 
staffing went from 9 to 6 employees and reported they have a WIA summer worker.  
r. Public Works and Planning and Zoning : Homero Vela reviewed the Public Works  and Planning and 
Zoning Department budgets. Supervisor Allen expressed her appreciation.  
s. Navajo County Flood Contro l District: Board of Directors’ Session: Supervisor Tenney made a 
motion to enter into the Navajo County Flood Control District Board of Directors ’ Session; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote unanimous approving the motion. Homero  Vela discussed 
the Navajo County Flood Control District  budget. The Board thanked Mr. Vela. Supervisor Tenney  
made a motion  to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
Chairman Nez, Supervisor Tenney, Supervisor Thompson, Supervisor Whitesinger and Supervisor Allen 
expressed appreciation to the elected officials and staff . Mr. Jayne thanked James Menlove and Paige Peterson. 
He also thanked Judge Ruechel for the meetings and find ing a compromise. Mr. Jayne thanked Homero Vela 
for all that he does for Navajo County.  
ADJOURN: At 5:06 p.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
 
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
Jonathan M. Nez, Chairman 
 
ATTEST:  
 
 
 
 
DATE:  June 11, 2013 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
